On Friday she\\n       donated three boxes of children's books to the school library.\\\").\\n       Template family: 6 multi-hop templates:\\n         M1: 'Who <verb>ed the <noun>?'           -> answer is a named entity\\n         M2: 'Did <entity> ever <verb> <noun>?'   -> answer YES/NO (boolean MR)\\n         M3: 'On what day did <entity> <verb>?'   -> answer is the named day (categorical)\\n         M4: 'Is it TRUE that <entity> <verb>ed at <place>?'  -> YES/NO\\n         M5: 'Did <entity> <verb> or <verb2>?'     -> answer is one of {verb, verb2}\\n         M6: 'Which of the following is true? A) ... B) ...'  -> 2-way MCQ\\n       Each story is parametric \u2014 entity names / verbs / nouns / places / days\\n       drawn from pools of >=30 each \u2014 and the story stores the underlying graph\\n       so the generator can derive the oracle for paraphrase, negation, and MCQ\\n       variants without needing an LLM.\\n       Variants per cluster:\\n         seed         : seed story + seed question (oracle answer derived from graph)\\n         paraphrase   : same graph, different surface nouns/verbs, identical oracle\\n         negation     : yes/no question that flips the seed label (e.g., seed asks\\n                        'Did Marisol buy the ledger?' -> negation asks 'Did Marisol\\n                        buy the ledger?' phrased as 'Is it NOT the case that Marisol\\n                        bought the ledger?' with oracle = NOT(seed_oracle))\\n         distractor   : swap one entity / day / place so the seed question becomes\\n                        factually wrong under the new distractor; oracle = opposite\\n                        of seed answer (this replaces the 'contrapositive' slot\\n                        with a real text-only MR that does not require FOL grounding).\\n\\n    (2b) First-order logic with quantifier-shift clusters (follows PrOntoQA / FOLIO precedent)\\n       Pool: 15 typed ontologies (e.g., 'Animals' -> {fido: dog, rex: dog,\\n       whiskers: cat, ...}, 'Friends' -> {alice, bob, carol, ...}).\\n       Template family: 8 FOL templates with all-quantifier / existential / negation:\\n         F1: 'All <type_A> are <type_B>. fido is a <type_A>. Therefore fido is a <type_B>.'  -> True\\n         F2: 'Some <type_A> are <type_B>. fido is a <type_A>. Therefore fido is a <type_B>.'  -> Uncertain\\n         F3: 'No <type_A> are <type_B>. fido is a <type_A>. Therefore fido is a <type_B>.'    -> False\\n         F4: 'All <type_A> are <type_B>. All <type_B> are <type_C>. Therefore all <type_A> are <type_C>.' -> True\\n         F5: 'All <type_A> are <type_B>. All <type_C> are <type_B>. Therefore some <type_A> are <type_C>.' -> Uncertain\\n         F6: 'Some <type_A> are <type_B>. All <type_B> are <type_C>. Therefore some <type_A> are <type_C>.' -> True\\n         F7: 'All <type_A> are not <type_B>. fido is a <type_A>. Therefore fido is not <type_B>.' -> True\\n         F8: 'All <type_A> are <type_B>. fido is not <type_B>. Therefore fido is not <type_A>.' -> False\\n       Variants per cluster:\\n         seed         : original template (True/False/Uncertain)\\n         paraphrase   : same template, fresh ontology entities, identical oracle\\n         negation     : question phrased as 'Is it NOT the case that <seed_conclusion>?'\\n                        oracle = flip(seed_oracle)\\n         quantifier_shift : swap 'All' <-> 'Some' on the major premise (referential\\n                        ambiguity is the source of difficulty; oracle = Uncertain\\n                        regardless of seed \u2014 this is a unique FOL-specific MR).\\n\\n    Rewire generate_mics(n_total, seed): split n_total evenly across 4 domains\\n    (logic, arithmetic, reading, FOL); keep the interleave shuffle. Domain tag stored\\n    in mic.domain; variant keys remain in {seed, paraphrase, negation, <4th>} where\\n    <4th> = {contrapositive | inverse | distractor | quantifier_shift}.\\n\\n    Add a self-test that verifies, for 20 random clusters per domain, that the\\n    oracle of seed == oracle of paraphrase, oracle of negation == flip(seed_oracle),\\n    and oracle of <4th> is consistent with the documented relation.\\n\\n(3) Reference-free arithmetic negation template\\n    File: mic_generator.py  -> _negation_arithmetic\\n    Current: 'Is the answer equal to <wrong>? Answer YES or NO.'   -> oracle NO.\\n    PROBLEM (reviewer-flagged): oracle requires knowing the seed answer to\\n    construct the wrong value, breaking the reference-free claim.\\n    REPLACEMENT templates (per-template dependent, choose deterministically\\n    based on the AS template id so the model cannot shortcut):\\n      AS1/AS3 (a+b): 'Is the answer greater than the first quantity?' oracle YES iff a+b>a\\n      AS2 (a-b):    'Is the answer greater than the second quantity?' oracle YES iff a-b>b\\n      AS4 (a-b+c):  'Is the answer less than the first quantity?'  oracle YES iff a-b+c<a\\n      AS5 (a+b):    'Is the answer greater than the second quantity?' oracle YES iff a+b>b\\n      AS6 (b-a):    'Is the answer greater than the amount paid?' oracle YES iff b-a>b\\n      AS7 (a/b):    'Is the answer at most 5?' oracle YES iff a/b<=5  (independent of a,b detail)\\n      AS8 ((a+b)/2):'Is the answer equal to <(a+b)/2 rounded to nearest int>?' oracle YES\\n      AS9/AS10/...: pick a structurally meaningful comparison\\n    The oracle is computed ONLY from quantities that appear in the question text;\\n    the seed_answer is no longer required to construct the negation template.\\n    Add a self-test asserting that for 50 random clusters the negation oracle\n    equals the model's expected YES/NO response when asked about the same predicate.\n\\n(4) Natural contamination via system-prompt injection\\n    File: scoring_grm.py  -> new function natural_contamination_simulation\\n    (replaces simulate_contamination for the v2 pipeline; keep the old function\\n    as a legacy 'response-overwrite' baseline so the contamination_comparison_report.md\\n    can plot both bars side by side).\\n    Procedure:\\n      (a) For each random seed in [0, 1, 42, 7, 99] (5 seeds, up from 3):\\n          - Pick 30% of clusters as 'contaminated'.\\n          - For each contaminated cluster, prepend a system message containing the\\n            seed oracle:\\n              CONTAMINATED SYSTEM PROMPT (template):\\n                'You have seen this question before. The correct answer is\\n                <oracle_for_seed_role_in_canonical_form>.'\\n            where the canonical form is: for logic -> 'V' or 'I'; for arithmetic\\n            -> the integer; for reading -> 'YES'/'NO'/entity/day; for FOL ->\\n            'TRUE'/'FALSE'/'UNCERTAIN'.\\n          - For each (target_model, contaminated_cluster, variant) in\\n            {seed, paraphrase, negation, <4th>}, call the existing OpenRouter\\n            client with the contaminated system prompt and the variant's user\\n            message; do NOT change the user message itself.\\n          - Recompute parse_for_domain, the raw 0..4 count per cluster, the ordinal\\n            score, and the GRM fit on the full (M, I) matrix.\\n      (b) Track a separate ledger counter contamination_spend_usd capped at $1.00.\\n      (c) For each seed, record:\\n            static_acc_clean, static_acc_contam, static_acc_delta,\\n            theta_clean, theta_contam, theta_delta, and a within_model_zscore\\n            = theta_delta / posterior_se_of_theta.\\n      (d) The success criterion is the within-model test: for the target_model,\\n          mean(theta_delta) <= +0.10 (theta stays bounded) AND mean(static_acc_delta)\\n          > 0.05 (static accuracy actually rises \u2014 i.e., the contamination\\n          injection landed). The natural-contamination result is also flagged\\n          with the fraction of contaminated clusters where the model's paraphrase/\\n          negation/<4th> ALSO flipped to the right answer (which would defeat M-IRT).\\n      (e) Run on TWO target models to harden the result: qwen/qwen-2.5-7b-instruct\\n          (same as iter-1) and meta-llama/llama-3.1-8b-instruct (new, lower-tier).\\n    Concurrency=10, requests_per_minute=12 (matches iter-1 rate cap).\\n\\n(5) Baseline consistency metrics\\n    File: scoring_grm.py  -> new function compute_consistency_baselines\\n    Computes per-model, on the same (M, I) response set:\\n      paraphrase_agreement_rate = (#clusters where parsed(seed) == parsed(paraphrase)) / I\\n                                  (in canonical response space per domain)\\n      negation_invariance_rate  = (#clusters where, in canonical form, the negation\\n                                   response is the relational inverse of the seed\\n                                   response) / I\\n        For logic: negation_invariant iff parsed(negation) == FLIP(parsed(seed))\\n        For arithmetic (yes/no negation): invariant iff parsed(negation) == 'NO'\\n        For reading: negation_invariant iff parsed(negation) == FLIP(parsed(seed))\\n        For FOL:    negation_invariant iff parsed(negation) == FLIP(parsed(seed))\\n      composite = mean(paraphrase_agreement_rate, negation_invariance_rate)\\n    Each metric is recorded alongside its theta and per-static-accuracy in\\n    method_out.json['baselines'] so the paper figures can plot them together.\\n\\n(6) K-ablation + LOO stability\\n    File: scoring_grm.py  -> new function grm_k_ablation\\n    Refit the GRM with K in {2, 3, 4, 5} on the same (M, I) raw score matrix.\\n    For each K, record:\\n      n_categories, log_likelihood, n_params, AIC, BIC,\\n      Pearson r against MMLU, Pearson r against HELM, Spearman \u03c1, Kendall's \u03c4,\\n      and a per-model theta vector.\\n    K=2 collapses to 2PL (dichotomous: top-grade vs not), K=3 is the iter-1 default\\n    (raw-> {0,1,2}), K=4 follows the research-report recommendation, K=5 adds an\\n    extra gradation (raw -> {0,1,2,3,4} or {0,1,2,3,>3}).\\n    The 'recommended K' is the one with the lowest BIC; report it explicitly.\\n\\n    File: scoring_grm.py  -> new function loo_theta_stability\\n    For each of the 15 models, drop that model's row from the (M, I) matrix,\\n    refit the GRM with the recommended K, and record the Pearson r of the\\n    remaining 14 \u03b8's vs the full-15 \u03b8's. Report per-model LOO \u0394\u03b8 as\\n    max(|loo_theta - full_theta|) across the 14 held-in models.\\n    This addresses the 'n_models = 5 is too few' critique from iter-1.\\n\\n(7) Four paper figures\\n    File: paper_figures.py  -> new top-level script (imported from method.py)\\n    Each figure is a matplotlib publication-quality plot, vector PDF + PNG,\\n    using the aii-data-fig-gen palette conventions:\\n      Figure 1 (pipeline diagram): horizontal flow boxes\\n        [Metamorphic Cluster Generator] -> [OpenRouter Query] -> [Coherence Scorer]\\n        -> [GRM Fit] -> [Theta / Baselines / Validation]\\n        Annotated with per-stage sample size (15 models x 4 domains x 100 MICs).\\n      Figure 2 (theta-vs-MMLU scatter): 15 points, one per model,\\n        x = MMLU (published), y = GRM theta (with the recommended K),\\n        overlay the linear-regression line and 95% CI band, report r in the title.\\n      Figure 3 (per-model coherence stacked-bar): x = 15 models,\\n        y = fraction of clusters in each ordinal category {0,1,2} (or {0..K-1} for\\n        the recommended K); stacked bars per model.\\n      Figure 4 (contamination Delta-theta bar plot):\\n        x = target_model (2 models), y = mean(theta_delta) within-model,\\n        error bars = std over seeds. Two bar groups per model:\\n          (a) iter-1 'response-overwrite' contamination (negative control \u2014 should\\n              show large |theta_delta|; demonstrates the iter-1 protocol is artificial).\\n          (b) v2 'natural contamination' via system-prompt injection (the v2 test;\\n              should show bounded |theta_delta| while static_acc_delta > 0).\\n    Each figure saves to paper_figures/figureN.{pdf,png} and embeds into the\\n    contamination_comparison_report.md as a Markdown link.\\n\\n--------------------------------------------------------------------------\\nLayout and CLI\\n--------------------------------------------------------------------------\\nmethod.py \u2014 orchestrator with --stage {all, generate, query, score, fit, validate,\\n                                     report, figures} and --mini flag.\\nFull default run:\\n  python method.py --stage all --n-clusters 400 --budget 4.0 --concurrency 10\\n  (400 MICs = 100 per domain; 15 models x 400 x 4 = 24000 calls;\\n   cap OpenRouter spend at $4 total, with a sub-cap of $1 for the natural-\\n   contamination re-queries).\\n\\nStage 1: generate_mics(n_total=400, seed=0)\\n  -> artifacts/exp_gen_sol_out.json     (datasets.examples[400])\\nStage 2: query_panel(panel=15, clusters, ledger=$3.50, concurrency=10)\\n  -> responses/<model>.jsonl             (15 files; 1600 records per file)\\nStage 3: score_all(clusters, responses, panel=15)\\n  -> artifacts/exp_eval_sol_out.json    (15 x 400 raw + ordinal matrices)\\nStage 3.5: compute_consistency_baselines() and static_baseline()\\n  -> in-memory; merged into method_out.json['baselines']\\nStage 4: fit_grm + grm_k_ablation(K=2,3,4,5) -> recommend K\\n  -> in-memory GRMResult\\nStage 5: validate_a(grm, panel) + LOO stability\\n  -> Pearson r + Spearman \u03c1 + Kendall's \u03c4 with 10,000-resample bootstrap CIs\\nStage 6: natural_contamination_simulation (target x2, seeds=5)\\n  -> artifacts/exp_sel_data_out.json (contamination_comparison_report + ledger)\\nStage 7: paper_figures.py (Figure 1-4)\\n  -> paper_figures/figure{1..4}.{pdf,png}\\nFinal: _build_formatted_variants() -> full/mini/preview method_out.json\\n\\n--------------------------------------------------------------------------\\nSchema-conformant outputs (validated by aii-json)\\n--------------------------------------------------------------------------\\n  full_method_out.json       : datasets[0].examples, 400 records\\n  mini_method_out.json       : first 10 records, strings capped at 200 chars\\n  preview_method_out.json    : first 3 records, strings capped at 80 chars\\n  artifacts/exp_gen_sol_out.json   : Stage 1 cluster metadata\\n  artifacts/exp_eval_sol_out.json  : Stage 3 per-model scoring\\n  artifacts/exp_sel_data_out.json  : Stage 6 contamination comparison\\n  paper_figures/figure1-4.{pdf,png}: Figure 1-4\\n  contamination_comparison_report.md : narrative summary + tables + Figure 4\\n\\n--------------------------------------------------------------------------\\nConcrete sub-budget allocation (~$1.50 total)\\n--------------------------------------------------------------------------\\nStage 2 base queries:   15 models x 400 MICs x 4 variants = 24000 calls\\n                        avg ~80 in + 8 out tokens\\n                        weighted by panel: ~$1.20\\nStage 6 contamination:  ~5 seeds x 2 targets x 30% of 400 x 4 = ~4800 calls\\n                        ~$0.30 (cap at $1.00)\\nTOTAL: ~$1.50 (well under the $10 per-artifact cap and the $3.50 main cap).\\n\\n--------------------------------------------------------------------------\\nFailure modes and ablations\\n--------------------------------------------------------------------------\\n* If a model id returns 404 from OpenRouter catalog fetch: drop it, log\\n  WARNING, continue with the remaining panel. If the panel drops below 10 models,\\n  fail loud (exit 2) \u2014 Pearson r on n<10 is unreliable.\\n* If a domain generator hits >80 retries (e.g., FOL generator can't satisfy the\\n  ontology constraint): halve that domain's MIC count and document it.\\n* If a contamination seed produces mean(theta_delta) > +0.50 for either target\\n  (the natural-contamination protocol fails on that seed), re-run with the next\\n  seed; document per-seed failures.\\n* If K=5 fit diverges (NaN theta): drop K=5 from the ablation table and report.\\n* If LOO std > 0.5 for any held-in model: flag it in the report and discuss in\\n  the contamination_comparison_report.md limitations section.\\n\",\n  \"fallback_plan\": \"Four escalation tiers. Trigger next tier ONLY if the prior tier cannot complete within remaining time/budget.\\n\\nTIER 1 (~10 min, library/model substitutions):\\n  * If a model id returns 404 from OpenRouter, swap via panel override:\\n      gpt-4o-mini        -> openai/gpt-4.1-mini\\n      gpt-4.1-mini       -> openai/gpt-oss-20b\\n      claude-haiku-4.5   -> anthropic/claude-3-5-haiku-20241022\\n      claude-sonnet-4    -> anthropic/claude-3-5-sonnet-20241022\\n      gemini-2.5-flash   -> google/gemini-2.0-flash-exp\\n      llama-3.1-8b       -> meta-llama/llama-3.2-3b-instruct\\n      llama-3.3-70b      -> meta-llama/llama-3.1-70b-instruct\\n      qwen-2.5-7b        -> qwen/qwen-2.5-3b-instruct\\n      qwen-2.5-72b       -> qwen/qwen-2.5-32b-instruct\\n      mistral-small-24b  -> mistralai/mistral-small-3.1-24b-instruct\\n      mistral-large-2    -> mistralai/mistral-large-latest\\n      deepseek-v3        -> deepseek/deepseek-chat\\n      phi-3.5-mini       -> microsoft/phi-3-mini-4k-instruct\\n      yi-1.5-9b          -> 01-ai/yi-1.5-6b-chat\\n      gemma-2-9b         -> google/gemma-2-2b-it\\n    Re-verify PUBLISHED_ACCOUNTURACY for each swapped id; record the swap date in the report.\\n  * If girth 0.8.0 EM fails on K=4 or K=5 (occasionally NaN for tiny n_models),\\n    drop to pyirt (pip install py-irt) and use the same fit() signature.\\n\\nTIER 2 (~30 min, hand-rolled GRM):\\n  Implement GRM from scratch in ~80 lines of NumPy with\\n    P(Y=k | theta, alpha, beta) = logistic(alpha*(theta-beta_{k-1})) - logistic(alpha*(theta-beta_k)),\\n    with beta_0=-inf and beta_K=+inf.  Marginal likelihood\\n    L_i(alpha_i, beta_i | x_i) = sum over j of integral of P(x_ij | theta_j) dPhi(theta_j; 0, 1),\\n    approximated by Gauss-Hermite quadrature with Q=41 nodes.\\n  EM: E-step computes posterior weights; M-step updates alpha, beta via L-BFGS-B\\n    with EAP-style regularization (priors from [6]: theta~N(0,1), alpha~LogNormal(0,0.5),\\n    beta_k~N(0,1) with beta_k < beta_{k+1}).  Stopping: |delta log L| < 1e-4 or 500 iters.\\n  Output identical schema to girth result.  Reference: Baker and Kim (2004),\\n  Item Response Theory: Parameter Estimation Techniques, 2nd ed., ch. 5.\\n\\nTIER 3 (~30 min, domain-drop degraded scope):\\n  Drop the new domain generators (multi-hop RC and FOL) and keep only the original\\n  2 domains (logic + arithmetic).  Reduce panel to 10 models (drop the 5 lowest-tier\\n  entries: phi-3.5-mini, yi-1.5-9b, gemma-2-9b, llama-3.3-70b, deepseek-v3).\\n  Reduce clusters to 200 (100 per domain).  Keep the K-ablation and the natural-\\n  contamination protocol but use only 1 target model.  Pearson r target relaxes to\\n  r > 0.80 with the lower CI bound > 0.75.\\n\\nTIER 4 (~60 min, severe degradation):\\n  Drop to 5 models and 100 clusters (50 per domain).  Skip the LOO analysis and the\\n  K=4 / K=5 ablation (keep K=2 and K=3 only).  Skip the natural-contamination\\n  re-query and report only the legacy 'response-overwrite' validation.  Pearson r\\n  target relaxes to r > 0.70 (matches iter-1 acceptance bar).  Document the scope\\n  limitations explicitly in contamination_comparison_report.md.\\n\\nAdditional safeguards:\\n  (1) Cumulative OpenRouter spend is tracked after EVERY call.  Hard stops:\\n        > $3.50 -> log WARNING, switch to :free tier models where available\\n        > $4.50 -> log CRITICAL, no more paraphrase/system-prompt calls\\n        > $5.00 -> raise SpendExceeded, persist partial output, exit 1\\n      Per-stage sub-caps: $3.50 base + $1.00 contamination = $4.50 hard cap.\\n  (2) If a domain generator produces <50 MICs after 80 retries, halve its target\\n      count and document in artifacts/method_out.json['domain_counts'].\\n  (3) If 3 of the 15 models return <50% parseable responses, the parse_for_domain\\n      regex is too strict; loosen INT_RE to allow decimals, then re-run.\\n  (4) If natural_contamination_simulation produces mean(static_acc_delta) < 0.02\\n      for a target model, the contamination injection failed (model ignored the\\n      system prompt); switch to 'user-message injection' (prepend oracle to the\\n      user message instead of system) and re-run; document the swap.\\n  (5) If K-ablation recommends K=2 (lowest BIC), log WARNING that the v2 results\\n      collapse to the iter-1 binary setup; document as a limitation.\\n  (6) If LOO analysis shows any model has |loo_theta - full_theta| > 1.0,\\n      flag that model as 'anchor point' (high-leverage observation) in the report.\\n\",\n  \"testing_plan\": \"Time budget: 6 h total.  Use mini -> 50 -> 200 -> 400 cluster scaling (per aii-long-running-tasks).  At each stage: validate output shape, check for crashes, record runtime, then extrapolate.  Wall-clock budget per stage: stage 1 ~5 min, stage 2 ~120 min (largest), stages 3-7 ~30 min combined.  OpenRouter spend ~$1.50 total.\\n\\nStage A \u2014 Generation (CPU-only, no API spend):\\n  A.1 (3 min) MINI: 8 MICs (2 per domain).  Checks: each cluster has exactly 4\\n      variants, all variant oracles are consistent with the relations dict, all\\n      domains are represented.  Hard assert: paraphrase oracle == seed oracle for\\n      all 8; negation oracle == flip(seed) for all 8.\\n  A.2 (5 min) 50 MICs (12-13 per domain): oracle validation on every cluster.\\n      Pass criterion: 100% consistency.\\n  A.3 (5 min) 200 MICs (50 per domain): oracle validation on 20% sample (40 clusters).\\n      Pass criterion: >=95% consistency.  If <95%, fix templates before proceeding.\\n  A.4 (5 min) Full 400 MICs: oracle validation on 50 (12.5%); >=95% pass.\\n      Persist to artifacts/exp_gen_sol_out.json.\\n\\nStage B \u2014 Model queries (API spend begins):\\n  B.1 (3 min) Smoke: 1 model (gpt-4o-mini) x 8 MICs (2 per domain).  Checks:\\n      HTTP 200; parsed response non-None for >=95% of calls; cost ledger records\\n      each call; system-prompt injection (Stage E) does not 403.\\n  B.2 (10 min) 50 MICs x 1 model x 4 variants = 200 calls; per-domain parse rate\\n      >= 90% for each domain.\\n  B.3 (20 min) 200 MICs x 3 models (1 closed + 2 open across the small/mid/large\\n      tiers): estimate cost per cluster x 400 x 15; verify estimate <= $1.50 base.\\n  B.4 (45 min) All 15 models x 200 MICs: compute coherence for all.  Pass criterion:\\n      theta ordering has at least 5 distinct values (not all collapsed to one).\\n  B.5 (60 min) All 15 models x 400 MICs x 4 variants = 24000 calls.  Cumulative\\n      spend <= $3.50; response matrix fully populated; per-model parse rate >= 80%.\\n\\nStage C \u2014 Scoring + Baselines (CPU, ~5 min):\\n  C.1 Compute the (15, 400) ordinal score matrix and the (15, 400) raw 0..4 matrix.\\n  C.2 Compute per-model summary; sanity check that per-model mean score is in [0.3, 1.7]\\n      (i.e., the response space is being used; not all 0 or all 2).\\n  C.3 Compute consistency baselines (paraphrase agreement, negation invariance,\\n      composite); sanity check that paraphrase_agreement_rate >= 0.50 for the top-3\\n      models (a 50% floor on a binary OR 4-way answer being self-consistent under\\n      paraphrase is what we expect for capable models).\\n\\nStage D \u2014 GRM fitting + K-ablation + LOO (CPU, ~5 min):\\n  D.1 Fit girth GRM with K=2, 3, 4, 5 on the (15, 400) raw score matrix.\\n    Checks: convergence in < 500 EM iterations for each K; all theta finite for each K.\\n  D.2 Compute AIC/BIC; recommend K (lowest BIC); log WARNING if K=2 wins.\\n  D.3 LOO stability: 15 fits, each dropping one model.  Pass: per-model\\n    |loo_theta - full_theta| <= 0.50 for at least 12 of 15 held-in models.\\n  D.4 Validation A: Pearson r(theta, MMLU) >= 0.85 with CI lower bound >= 0.85.\\n    If lower bound < 0.85, recheck AIC and consider recommending K=2 only as a\\n    robustness check (not a fail).\\n\\nStage E \u2014 Natural contamination (API spend resumes):\\n  E.1 (3 min) Smoke: qwen-2.5-7b-instruct x 8 contaminated MICs x 4 variants x 1 seed\\n      = 32 calls.  Checks: contaminated system prompt accepted by OpenRouter;\\n      static_acc on contaminated subset > static_acc on clean subset.\\n  E.2 (15 min) Natural contamination full pass: qwen-2.5-7b-instruct x 5 seeds\\n      (target 120 contaminated clusters, 4 variants each = 480 calls).\\n  E.3 (15 min) Same for meta-llama/llama-3.1-8b-instruct.\\n  E.4 Pass criterion: for each target, mean(static_acc_delta) > 0.05 AND\\n      mean(theta_delta) <= +0.10 (within-model comparison).  Cumulative spend\\n      <= $1.00.\\n  E.5 Bonus check: fraction of contaminated clusters where paraphrase/negation/<4th>\\n      ALSO went to the right answer (would defeat M-IRT).  Report this fraction;\\n      if > 0.30, log WARNING that the natural-contamination test is suspect.\\n\\nStage F \u2014 Figures + Final assembly (~10 min):\\n  F.1 Generate Figure 1 (pipeline diagram) \u2014 concept-style boxes; no data.\\n  F.2 Generate Figure 2 (theta-vs-MMLU scatter) \u2014 15 points + 95% CI band.\\n  F.3 Generate Figure 3 (per-model coherence stacked-bar) \u2014 15 stacked bars.\\n  F.4 Generate Figure 4 (contamination Delta-theta bar plot) \u2014 2 targets x 2 protocols.\\n  F.5 Validate method_out.json against the repo schema via aii-json.\\n  F.6 Generate full/mini/preview method_out.json variants.\\n  F.7 Write contamination_comparison_report.md (tables + Figure 4 inline + limitations).\\n  F.8 Re-run the entire pipeline once more from scratch to confirm reproducibility;\\n      verify |delta theta_i| < 0.10, |delta r| < 0.05 across the 15 models.\\n\\nConfirmation signals (the experiment is on track if, by the end of Stage D.4):\\n  * Theta ordering matches qualitative MMLU ordering for at least 12 of 15 models\\n    (Spearman \u03c1 >= 0.80 between theta ranking and MMLU ranking).\\n  * Per-model static accuracy on seeds in [0.4, 0.95] (pipeline is informative).\\n  * Coherence scores span at least 2 of 3 ordinal categories for at least 10 of 15\\n    models (item-response space is being used).\\n  * K-ablation recommends K=3 or K=4 (NOT K=2 \u2014 that would mean the polytomous\\n    model adds nothing over 2PL).\\n  * K=2 ablation has lower BIC than K=3 -> log WARNING; Reference: Baker and Kim (2004),\\n  Item Response Theory: Parameter Estimation Techniques, 2nd ed., ch. 5.\\n\\nTIER 3 (~30 min, domain-drop degraded scope):\\n  Drop the new domain generators (multi-hop RC and FOL) and keep only the original\\n  2 domains (logic + arithmetic). Keep the K-ablation and the natural-\\n  contamination protocol but use only 1 target model.  Pearson r target relaxes to\\n  r > 0.80 with the lower CI bound > 0.75.\\n\\nTIER 4 (~60 min, severe degradation):\\n  Drop to 5 models and 100 clusters (50 per domain).  Skip the LOO analysis and the\\n  K=4 / K=5 ablation (keep K=2 and K=3 only).  Skip the natural-contamination\\n  re-query and report only the legacy 'response-overwrite' validation.  Pearson r\\n  target relaxes to r > 0.70 (matches iter-1 acceptance bar).  Document the scope\\n  limitations explicitly in contamination_comparison_report.md.\\n\\nAdditional safeguards:\\n  (1) Cumulative OpenRouter spend is tracked after EVERY call.  Hard stops:\\n        > $3.50 -> log WARNING, switch to :free tier models where available\\n        > $4.50 -> log CRITICAL, no more paraphrase/system-prompt calls\\n        > $5.00 -> raise SpendExceeded, persist partial output, exit 1\\n      Per-stage sub-caps: $3.50 base + $1.00 contamination = $4.50 hard cap.\\n  (2) If a domain generator produces <50 MICs after 80 retries, halve its target\\n      count and document in artifacts/method_out.json['domain_counts'].\\n  (3) If 3 of the 15 models return <50% parseable responses, the parse_for_domain\\n      regex is too strict; loosen INT_RE to allow decimals, then re-run.\\n  (4) If natural_contamination_simulation produces mean(static_acc_delta) < 0.02\\n      for a target model, the contamination injection failed (model ignored the\\n      system prompt); switch to 'user-message injection' (prepend oracle to the\\n      user message instead of system) and re-run; document the swap.\\n  (5) If K-ablation recommends K=2 (lowest BIC), log WARNING that the v2 results\\n      collapse to the iter-1 binary setup; document as a limitation.\\n  (6) If LOO analysis shows any model has |loo_theta - full_theta| > 1.0,\\n      flag that model as 'anchor point' (high-leverage observation) in the report.\\n\",\n  \"testing_plan\": \"Time budget: 6 h total.  Use mini -> 50 -> 200 -> 400 cluster scaling (per aii-long-running-tasks).  At each stage: validate output shape, check for crashes, record runtime, then extrapolate.  Wall-clock budget per stage: stage 1 ~5 min, stage 2 ~120 min (largest), stages 3-7 ~30 min combined.  OpenRouter spend ~$1.50 total.\\n\\nStage A \u2014 Generation (CPU-only, no API spend):\\n  A.1 (3 min) MINI: 8 MICs (2 per domain).  Checks: each cluster has exactly 4\\n      variants, all variant oracles are consistent with the relations dict, all\\n      domains are represented.  Hard assert: paraphrase oracle == seed oracle for\\n      all 8; negation oracle == flip(seed) for all 8.\\n  A.2 (5 min) 50 MICs (12-13 per domain): oracle validation on every cluster.\\n      Pass criterion: 100% consistency.\\n  A.3 (5 min) 200 MICs (50 per domain): oracle validation on 20% sample (40 clusters).\\n      Pass criterion: >=95% consistency.  If <95%, fix templates before proceeding.\\n  A.4 (5 min) Full 400 MICs: oracle validation on 50 (12.5%); >=95% pass.\\n      Persist to artifacts/exp_gen_sol_out.json.\\n\\nStage B \u2014 Model queries (API spend begins):\\n  B.1 (3 min) Smoke: 1 model (gpt-4o-mini) x 8 MICs (2 per domain).  Checks:\\n      HTTP 200; parsed response non-None for >=95% of calls; cost ledger records\\n      each call; system-prompt injection (Stage E) does not 403.\\n  B.2 (10 min) 50 MICs x 1 model x 4 variants = 200 calls; per-domain parse rate\\n      >= 90% for each domain.\\n  B.3 (20 min) 200 MICs x 3 models (1 closed + 2 open across the small/mid/large\\n      tiers): estimate cost per cluster x 400 x 15; verify estimate <= $1.50 base.\\n  B.4 (45 min) All 15 models x 200 MICs: compute coherence for all.  Pass criterion:\\n      theta ordering has at least 5 distinct values (not all collapsed to one).\\n  B.5 (60 min) All 15 models x 400 MICs x 4 variants = 24000 calls.  Cumulative\\n      spend <= $3.50; response matrix fully populated; per-model parse rate >= 80%.\\n\\nStage C \u2014 Scoring + Baselines (CPU, ~5 min):\\n  C.1 Compute the (15, 400) ordinal score matrix and the (15, 400) raw 0..4 matrix.\\n  C.2 Compute per-model summary; sanity check that per-model mean score is in [0.3, 1.7]\\n      (i.e., the response space is being used; not all 0 or all 2).\\n  C.3 Compute consistency baselines (paraphrase agreement, negation invariance,\\n      composite); sanity check that paraphrase_agreement_rate >= 0.50 for the top-3\\n      models (a 50% floor on a binary OR 4-way answer being self-consistent under\\n      paraphrase is what we expect for capable models).\\n\\nStage D \u2014 GRM fitting + K-ablation + LOO (CPU, ~5 min):\\n  D.1 Fit girth GRM with K=2, 3, 4, 5 on the (15, 400) raw score matrix.\\n    Checks: convergence in < 500 EM iterations for each K; all theta finite for each K.\\n  D.2 Compute AIC/BIC; recommend K (lowest BIC); log WARNING if K=2 wins.\\n  D.3 LOO stability: 15 fits, each dropping one model.  Pass: per-model\\n    |loo_theta - full_theta| <= 0.50 for at least 12 of 15 held-in models.\\n  D.4 Validation A: Pearson r(theta, MMLU) >= 0.85 with CI lower bound >= 0.85.\\n    If lower bound < 0.85, recheck AIC and consider recommending K=2 only as a\\n    robustness check (not a fail).\\n\\nStage E \u2014 Natural contamination (API spend resumes):\\n  E.1 (3 min) Smoke: qwen-2.5-7b-instruct x 8 contaminated MICs x 4 variants x 1 seed\\n      = 32 calls.  Checks: contaminated system prompt accepted by OpenRouter;\\n      static_acc on contaminated subset > static_acc on clean subset.\\n  E.2 (15 min) Natural contamination full pass: qwen-2.5-7b-instruct x 5 seeds\\n      (target 120 contaminated clusters, 4 variants each = 480 calls).\\n  E.3 (15 min) Same for meta-llama/llama-3.1-8b-instruct.\\n  E.4 Pass criterion: for each target, mean(static_acc_delta) > 0.05 AND\\n      mean(theta_delta) <= +0.10 (within-model comparison).  Cumulative spend\\n      <= $1.00.\\n  E.5 Bonus check: fraction of contaminated clusters where paraphrase/negation/<4th>\\n      ALSO went to the right answer (would defeat M-IRT).  Report this fraction;\\n      if > 0.30, log WARNING that the natural-contamination test is suspect.\\n\\nStage F \u2014 Figures + Final assembly (~10 min):\\n  F.1 Generate Figure 1 (pipeline diagram) \u2014 concept-style boxes; no data.\\n  F.2 Generate Figure 2 (theta-vs-MMLU scatter) \u2014 15 points + 95% CI band.\\n  F.3 Generate Figure 3 (per-model coherence stacked-bar) \u2014 15 stacked bars.\\n  F.4 Generate Figure 4 (contamination Delta-theta bar plot) \u2014 2 targets x 2 protocols.\\n  F.5 Validate method_out.json against the repo schema via aii-json.\\n  F.6 Generate full/mini/preview method_out.json variants.\\n  F.7 Write contamination_comparison_report.md (tables + Figure 4 inline + limitations).\\n  F.8 Re-run the entire pipeline once more from scratch to confirm reproducibility;\\n      verify |delta theta_i| < 0.10, |delta r| < 0.05 across the 15 models.\\n\\nConfirmation signals (the experiment is on track if, by the end of Stage D.4):\\n  * Theta ordering matches qualitative MMLU ordering for at least 12 of 15 models\\n    (Spearman \u03c1 >= 0.80 between theta ranking and MMLU ranking).\\n  * Per-model static accuracy on seeds in [0.4, 0.95] (pipeline is informative).\\n  * Coherence scores span at least 2 of 3 ordinal categories for at least 10 of 15\\n    models (item-response space is being used).\\n  * K-ablation recommends K=3 or K=4 (NOT K=2 \u2014 that would mean the polytomous\\n    model adds nothing over 2PL).\\n  * K=2 ablation has lower BIC than K=3 -> log WARNING; consider re-running with\\n    a stricter coherence mapping or larger cluster count.\\n  * Natural contamination produces within-model mean(theta_delta) <= +0.10 while\\n    mean(static_acc_delta) > 0.05 on at least one of the two target models.\\n  * Both contamination targets show |theta_delta| < |response_overwrite_theta_delta|\\n    (i.e., natural contamination is less damaging than the iter-1 artificial one \u2014\\n    this validates the v2 protocol design).\\nIf any signal is missing, the most likely cause is response normalization (Stage B.1);\\ndebug that first before scaling.\\n\\nCost guardrails:\\n  * Cumulative OpenRouter spend tracked after every call.  Hard stops:\\n      > $3.50 -> log WARNING, switch to cheapest-tier models\\n      > $4.50 -> log CRITICAL, no more contamination calls\\n      > $5.00 -> raise SpendExceeded, persist partial output, exit 1\\n  * Per-stage sub-caps: $3.50 base queries + $1.00 contamination = $4.50 hard cap.\\n  * Total budgeted: $1.20 (Stage B) + $0.30 (Stage E) + $0.30 buffer = $1.80.\\n\"\n}", "kind": "FileEditorObservation"}

B) ...'  -> 2-way MCQ\\n       Each story is parametric \u2014 entity names / verbs / nouns / places / days\\n       drawn from pools of >=30 each \u2014 and the story stores the underlying graph\\n       so the generator can derive the oracle for paraphrase, negation, and MCQ\\n       variants without needing an LLM.\\n       Variants per cluster:\\n         seed         : seed story + seed question (oracle answer derived from graph)\\n         paraphrase   : same graph, different surface nouns/verbs, identical oracle\\n         negation     : yes/no question that flips the seed label (e.g., seed asks\\n                        'Did Marisol buy the ledger?' -> negation asks 'Did Marisol\\n                        buy the ledger?' phrased as 'Is it NOT the case that Marisol\\n                        bought the ledger?' with oracle = NOT(seed_oracle))\\n         distractor   : swap one entity / day / place so the seed question becomes\\n                        factually wrong under the new distractor; oracle = opposite\\n                        of seed answer (this replaces the 'contrapositive' slot\\n                        with a real text-only MR that does not require FOL grounding).\\n\\n    (2b) First-order logic with quantifier-shift clusters (follows PrOntoQA / FOLIO precedent)\\n       Pool: 15 typed ontologies (e.g., 'Animals' -> {fido: dog, rex: dog,\\n       whiskers: cat, ...}, 'Friends' -> {alice, bob, carol, ...}).\\n       Template family: 8 FOL templates with all-quantifier / existential / negation:\\n         F1: 'All <type_A> are <type_B>. fido is a <type_A>. Therefore fido is a <type_B>.'  -> Uncertain\\n         F3: 'No <type_A> are <type_B>. fido is a <type_A>. Therefore fido is a <type_B>.'    -> False\\n         F4: 'All <type_A> are <type_B>. Therefore some <type_A> are <type_C>.' -> Uncertain\\n         F6: 'Some <type_A> are <type_B>. All <type_B> are <type_C>. Therefore some <type_A> are <type_C>.' -> True\\n         F7: 'All <type_A> are not <type_B>. fido is a <type_A>. Therefore fido is not <type_B>.' -> True\\n         F8: 'All <type_A> are <type_B>. fido is not <type_B>. A) ... B) ...'  -> 2-way MCQ\\n       Each story is parametric \u2014 entity names / verbs / nouns / places / days\\n       drawn from pools of >=30 each \u2014 and the story stores the underlying graph\\n       so the generator can derive the oracle for paraphrase, negation, and MCQ\\n       variants without needing an LLM.\\n       Variants per cluster:\\n         seed         : seed story + seed question (oracle answer derived from graph)\\n         paraphrase   : same graph, different surface nouns/verbs, identical oracle\\n         negation     : yes/no question that flips the seed label (e.g., seed asks\\n                        'Did Marisol buy the ledger?' -> negation asks 'Did Marisol\\n                        buy the ledger?' phrased as 'Is it NOT the case that Marisol\\n                        bought the ledger?' with oracle = NOT(seed_oracle))\\n         distractor   : swap one entity / day / place so the seed question becomes\\n                        factually wrong under the new distractor; oracle = opposite\\n                        of seed answer (this replaces the 'contrapositive' slot\\n                        with a real text-only MR that does not require FOL grounding).\\n\\n    (2b) First-order logic with quantifier-shift clusters (follows PrOntoQA / FOLIO precedent)\\n       Pool: 15 typed ontologies (e.g., 'Animals' -> {fido: dog, rex: dog,\\n       whiskers: cat, ...}, 'Friends' -> {alice, bob, carol, ...}).\\n       Template family: 8 FOL templates with all-quantifier / existential / negation:\\n         F1: 'All <type_A> are <type_B>. fido is a <type_A>. If the panel drops below 10 models,\\n  fail loud (exit 2) \u2014 Pearson r on n<10 is unreliable.\\n* If a domain generator hits >80 retries (e.g., FOL generator can't satisfy the\\n  ontology constraint): halve that domain's MIC count and document it.\\n* If a contamination seed produces mean(theta_delta) > +0.50 for either target\\n  (the natural-contamination protocol fails on that seed), re-run with the next\\n  seed; document per-seed failures.\\n* If K=5 fit diverges (NaN theta): drop K=5 from the ablation table and report.\\n* If LOO std > 0.5 for any held-in model: flag it in the report and discuss in\\n  the contamination_comparison_report.md limitations section.\\n\",\n     6\t  \"fallback_plan\": \"Four escalation tiers. Trigger next tier ONLY if the prior tier cannot complete within remaining time/budget.\\n\\nTIER 1 (~10 min, library/model substitutions):\\n  * If a model id returns 404 from OpenRouter, swap via panel override:\\n      gpt-4o-mini        -> openai/gpt-4.1-mini\\n      gpt-4.1-mini       -> openai/gpt-oss-20b\\n      claude-haiku-4.5   -> anthropic/claude-3-5-haiku-20241022\\n      claude-sonnet-4    -> anthropic/claude-3-5-sonnet-20241022\\n      gemini-2.5-flash   -> google/gemini-2.0-flash-exp\\n      llama-3.1-8b       -> meta-llama/llama-3.2-3b-instruct\\n      llama-3.3-70b      -> meta-llama/llama-3.1-70b-instruct\\n      qwen-2.5-7b        -> qwen/qwen-2.5-3b-instruct\\n      qwen-2.5-72b       -> qwen/qwen-2.5-32b-instruct\\n      mistral-small-24b  -> mistralai/mistral-small-3.1-24b-instruct\\n      mistral-large-2    -> mistralai/mistral-large-latest\\n      deepseek-v3        -> deepseek/deepseek-chat\\n      phi-3.5-mini       -> microsoft/phi-3-mini-4k-instruct\\n      yi-1.5-9b          -> 01-ai/yi-1.5-6b-chat\\n      gemma-2-9b         -> google/gemma-2-2b-it\\n    Re-verify PUBLISHED_ACCOUNTURACY for each swapped id; record the swap date in the report.\\n  * If girth 0.8.0 EM fails on K=4 or K=5 (occasionally NaN for tiny n_models),\\n    drop to pyirt (pip install py-irt) and use the same fit() signature.\\n\\nTIER 2 (~30 min, hand-rolled GRM):\\n  Implement GRM from scratch in ~80 lines of NumPy with\\n    P(Y=k | theta, alpha, beta) = logistic(alpha*(theta-beta_{k-1})) - logistic(alpha*(theta-beta_k)),\\n    with beta_0=-inf and beta_K=+inf.  Marginal likelihood\\n    L_i(alpha_i, beta_i | x_i) = sum over j of integral of P(x_ij | theta_j) dPhi(theta_j; 0, 1),\\n    approximated by Gauss-Hermite quadrature with Q=41 nodes.\\n  EM: E-step computes posterior weights; M-step updates alpha, beta via L-BFGS-B\\n    with EAP-style regularization (priors from [6]: theta~N(0,1), alpha~LogNormal(0,0.5),\\n    beta_k~N(0,1) with beta_k < beta_{k+1}).  Stopping: |delta log L| < 1e-4 or 500 iters.\\n  Output identical schema to girth result.  Reference: Baker and Kim (2004),\\n  Item Response Theory: Parameter Estimation Techniques, 2nd ed., ch. 5.\\n\\nTIER 3 (~30 min, domain-drop degraded scope):\\n  Drop the new domain generators (multi-hop RC and FOL) and keep only the original\\n  2 domains (logic + arithmetic).  Reduce panel to 10 models (drop the 5 lowest-tier\\n  entries: phi-3.5-mini, yi-1.5-9b, gemma-2-9b, llama-3.3-70b, deepseek-v3).\\n  Reduce clusters to 200 (100 per domain).  Keep the K-ablation and the natural-\\n  contamination protocol but use only 1 target model.  Pearson r target relaxes to\\n  r > 0.80 with the lower CI bound > 0.75.\\n\\nTIER 4 (~60 min, severe degradation):\\n  Drop to 5 models and 100 clusters (50 per domain).  Skip the LOO analysis and the\\n  K=4 / K=5 ablation (keep K=2 and K=3 only).  Skip the natural-contamination\\n  re-query and report only the legacy 'response-overwrite' validation.  Pearson r\\n  target relaxes to r > 0.70 (matches iter-1 acceptance bar).  Document the scope\\n  limitations explicitly in contamination_comparison_report.md.\\n\\nAdditional safeguards:\\n  (1) Cumulative OpenRouter spend is tracked after EVERY call.  Hard stops:\\n        > $3.50 -> log WARNING, switch to :free tier models where available\\n        > $4.50 -> log CRITICAL, no more paraphrase/system-prompt calls\\n        > $5.00 -> raise SpendExceeded, persist partial output, exit 1\\n      Per-stage sub-caps: $3.50 base + $1.00 contamination = $4.50 hard cap.\\n  (2) If a domain generator produces <50 MICs after 80 retries, halve its target\\n      count and document in artifacts/method_out.json['domain_counts'].\\n  (3) If 3 of the 15 models return <50% parseable responses, the parse_for_domain\\n      regex is too strict; loosen INT_RE to allow decimals, then re-run.\\n  (4) If natural_contamination_simulation produces mean(static_acc_delta) < 0.02\\n      for a target model, the contamination injection failed (model ignored the\\n      system prompt); switch to 'user-message injection' (prepend oracle to the\\n      user message instead of system) and re-run; document the swap.\\n  (5) If K-ablation recommends K=2 (lowest BIC), log WARNING that the v2 results\\n      collapse to the iter-1 binary setup; document as a limitation.\\n  (6) If LOO analysis shows any model has |loo_theta - full_theta| > 1.0,\\n      flag that model as 'anchor point' (high-leverage observation) in the report.\\n\",\n"}], "is_error": false, "command": "view", "path": "/ai-inventor/aii_data/runs/run_56IKXpJEZJ5b/3_invention_loop/iter_2/gen_plan/gen_plan_experiment_1/.sdk_openhands_agent_struct_out.json", "prev_exist": true, "old_content": null, "new_content": null, "kind": "FileEditorObservation"}

open, small + large, 4+ families (gpt-4o-mini, gpt-4.1-mini, claude-haiku-4.5, claude-sonnet-4, gemini-2.5-flash, llama-3.1-8b, llama-3.3-70b, qwen-2.5-7b, qwen-2.5-72b, mistral-small-24b, mistral-large-2, deepseek-v3, phi-3.5-mini, yi-1.5-9b, gemma-2-9b)\n2. **Two new domain generators** \u2014 multi-hop reading comprehension (LGMT-style entity-graph stories) and first-order logic with quantifier-shift clusters (PrOntoQA/FOLIO precedents)\n3. **Reference-free arithmetic negation** \u2014 per-template predicates ('Is the answer greater than the first quantity?') so the negation oracle is derivable from question text alone\n4. **Natural contamination** via system-prompt injection of the seed oracle on a 30% subset of clusters, re-querying all four variants, on two target models\n5. **Three baseline consistency metrics** (paraphrase agreement, negation invariance, composite) computed on the same clusters with Pearson r, Spearman \u03c1, Kendall's \u03c4 vs MMLU/HELM and 10,000-resample bootstrap CIs\n6. **K\u2208{2,3,4,5} AIC/BIC ablation** plus leave-one-out \u03b8 stability across the 15 models\n7. **Four paper figures** \u2014 pipeline diagram, \u03b8-vs-MMLU scatter with 15 points + 95% CI band, per-model coherence stacked-bar, and contamination \u0394\u03b8 bar plot with both iter-1 'response-overwrite' and v2 'natural contamination' protocols side by side\n\nThe plan preserves the iter-1 stage structure (generate \u2192 query \u2192 score \u2192 fit \u2192 validate \u2192 report), reuses the existing files (mic_generator.py, scoring_grm.py, openrouter_client.py, method.py, pyproject.toml), and adds three new functions (compute_consistency_baselines, grm_k_ablation, loo_theta_stability) plus a new natural_contamination_simulation that replaces the iter-1 'response-overwrite' protocol. Output schema is exp_gen_sol_out / exp_eval_sol_out / exp_sel_data_out / method_out.json (full/mini/preview) plus paper_figures/figure{1..4}.{pdf,png} and contamination_comparison_report.md.\n\n**Budget**: ~$1.50 OpenRouter spend ($1.20 Stage 2 base + $0.30 Stage 6 contamination), well under the $10 per-artifact cap and the $4.50 hard cap.\n**Wall-clock**: ~2.5\u20133 h at concurrency=10, fitting in the 6h executor budget.\n**Compute profile**: cpu_light (girth GRM fits, NumPy bootstrap CIs, no GPU needed).\n\nThe four-tier fallback_plan covers library failure (pyirt / hand-rolled GRM), domain-drop degraded scope, and severe degradation to 5 models / 100 clusters / 2-domain-only mode. The testing_plan uses mini \u2192 50 \u2192 200 \u2192 400 cluster scaling with explicit confirmation signals at each stage (theta ranking, static accuracy range, K-ablation recommendation, natural-contamination effect on \u0394\u03b8)."

abort the run if a model id is unknown.\\n\\n(2) Two new domain generators (multi-hop reading comprehension + first-order logic)\\n    File: mic_generator.py -> add two new builders; rewire generate_mics() signature\\n\\n    (2a) Multi-hop reading comprehension (follows the LGMT precedent the research artifact cites)\\n       Pool: 25 short 4-sentence stories about a fictional entity (e.g., Marisol owns a small bookstore in Quito. Each morning she walks her dog Pepper along the river. Last Tuesday she bought a new ledger from the stationer. On Friday she donated three boxes of children's books to the school library.).\\n       Template family: 6 multi-hop templates:\\n         M1: 'Who <verb>ed the <noun>?'                          -> answer is a named entity\\n         M2: 'Did <entity> ever <verb> <noun>?'                  -> answer YES/NO (boolean MR)\\n         M3: 'On what day did <entity> <verb>?'                  -> answer is the named day (categorical)\\n         M4: 'Is it TRUE that <entity> <verb>ed at <place>?'    -> YES/NO\\n         M5: 'Did <entity> <verb> or <verb2>?'                   -> answer is one of {verb, verb2}\\n         M6: 'Which of the following is true? A) ... B) ...'    -> 2-way MCQ\\n       Each story is parametric -- entity names / verbs / nouns / places / days drawn from pools of >=30 each -- and the story stores the underlying graph so the generator can derive the oracle for paraphrase, negation, and MCQ variants without needing an LLM.\\n       Variants per cluster:\\n         seed         : seed story + seed question (oracle derived from graph)\\n         paraphrase   : same graph, different surface nouns/verbs, identical oracle\\n         negation     : yes/no question that flips the seed label\\n         distractor   : swap one entity / day / place so the seed question becomes factually wrong; oracle = opposite of seed answer\\n\\n    (2b) First-order logic with quantifier-shift clusters (follows PrOntoQA / FOLIO precedent)\\n       Pool: 15 typed ontologies (e.g., Animals -> {fido: dog, rex: dog, whiskers: cat}, Friends -> {alice, bob, carol}).\\n       Template family: 8 FOL templates with all-quantifier / existential / negation:\\n         F1: 'All <A> are <B>. fido is a <A>. Therefore fido is a <B>.' -> True\\n         F2: 'Some <A> are <B>. fido is a <A>. Therefore fido is a <B>.' -> Uncertain\\n         F3: 'No <A> are <B>. fido is a <A>. Therefore fido is a <B>.' -> False\\n         F4: 'All <A> are <B>. All <B> are <C>. Therefore all <A> are <C>.' -> True\\n         F5: 'All <A> are <B>. All <C> are <B>. Therefore some <A> are <C>.' -> Uncertain\\n         F6: 'Some <A> are <B>. All <B> are <C>. Therefore some <A> are <C>.' -> True\\n         F7: 'All <A> are not <B>. fido is a <A>. Therefore fido is not <B>.' -> True\\n         F8: 'All <A> are <B>. fido is not <B>. Therefore fido is not <A>.' -> False\\n       Variants per cluster:\\n         seed             : original template (True/False/Uncertain)\\n         paraphrase       : same template, fresh ontology entities, identical oracle\\n         negation         : 'Is it NOT the case that <seed_conclusion>?' oracle = flip(seed_oracle)\\n         quantifier_shift : swap 'All' <-> 'Some' on the major premise; oracle = Uncertain regardless of seed.\\n\\n    Rewire generate_mics(n_total, seed): split n_total evenly across 4 domains (logic, arithmetic, reading, FOL); keep the interleave shuffle. Domain tag stored in mic.domain; variant keys remain in {seed, paraphrase, negation, <4th>} where <4th> = {contrapositive | inverse | distractor | quantifier_shift}.\\n\\n    Add a self-test that verifies, for 20 random clusters per domain, that the oracle of seed == oracle of paraphrase, oracle of negation == flip(seed_oracle), and oracle of <4th> is consistent with the documented relation.\\n\\n(3) Reference-free arithmetic negation template\\n    File: mic_generator.py -> _negation_arithmetic\\n    Current: 'Is the answer equal to <wrong>? Answer YES or NO.' -> oracle NO.\\n    PROBLEM (reviewer-flagged): oracle requires knowing the seed answer to construct the wrong value, breaking the reference-free claim.\\n    REPLACEMENT templates (per-template dependent, chosen deterministically based on the AS template id):\\n      AS1/AS3 (a+b): 'Is the answer greater than the first quantity?'  oracle YES iff a+b > a\\n      AS2 (a-b):    'Is the answer greater than the second quantity?' oracle YES iff a-b > b\\n      AS4 (a-b+c):  'Is the answer less than the first quantity?'     oracle YES iff a-b+c < a\\n      AS5 (a+b):    'Is the answer greater than the second quantity?' oracle YES iff a+b > b\\n      AS6 (b-a):    'Is the answer greater than the amount paid?'     oracle YES iff b-a > b\\n      AS7 (a/b):    'Is the answer at most 5?'                        oracle YES iff a/b <= 5 (independent of a,b detail)\\n      AS8 ((a+b)/2):'Is the answer even?'                             oracle YES iff (a+b)/2 is even\\n    The oracle is computed ONLY from quantities that appear in the question text; the seed_answer is no longer required to construct the negation template.\\n    Add a self-test asserting that for 50 random clusters the negation oracle equals the model's expected YES/NO response when asked about the same predicate.\\n\\n(4) Natural contamination via system-prompt injection\\n    File: scoring_grm.py -> new function natural_contamination_simulation\\n    (replaces simulate_contamination for the v2 pipeline; keep the old function as a legacy 'response-overwrite' baseline so the contamination_comparison_report.md can plot both bars side by side).\\n    Procedure:\\n      (a) For each random seed in [0, 1, 42, 7, 99] (5 seeds, up from 3):\\n          - Pick 30 percent of clusters as 'contaminated'.\\n          - For each contaminated cluster, prepend a system message containing the seed oracle:\\n              CONTAMINATED SYSTEM PROMPT (template):\\n                'You have seen this question before. The correct answer is <oracle_for_seed_role_in_canonical_form>.'\\n            where the canonical form is: for logic -> 'V' or 'I'; for arithmetic -> the integer; for reading -> 'YES'/'NO'/entity/day; for FOL -> 'TRUE'/'FALSE'/'UNCERTAIN'.\\n          - For each (target_model, contaminated_cluster, variant) in {seed, paraphrase, negation, <4th>}, call the existing OpenRouter client with the contaminated system prompt and the variant's user message; do NOT change the user message itself.\\n          - Recompute parse_for_domain, the raw 0..4 count per cluster, the ordinal score, and the GRM fit on the full (M, I) matrix.\\n      (b) Track a separate ledger counter contamination_spend_usd capped at $1.00.\\n      (c) For each seed, record:\\n            static_acc_clean, static_acc_contam, static_acc_delta,\\n            theta_clean, theta_contam, theta_delta, and a within_model_zscore = theta_delta / posterior_se_of_theta.\\n      (d) The success criterion is the within-model test: for the target_model, mean(theta_delta) <= +0.10 (theta stays bounded) AND mean(static_acc_delta) > 0.05 (static accuracy actually rises -- the contamination injection landed).\\n      (e) Run on TWO target models to harden the result: qwen/qwen-2.5-7b-instruct (same as iter-1) and meta-llama/llama-3.1-8b-instruct (new, lower-tier).\\n    Concurrency=10, requests_per_minute=12 (matches iter-1 rate cap).\\n\\n(5) Baseline consistency metrics\\n    File: scoring_grm.py -> new function compute_consistency_baselines\\n    Computes per-model, on the same (M, I) response set:\\n      paraphrase_agreement_rate = (#clusters where parsed(seed) == parsed(paraphrase)) / I  (in canonical response space per domain)\\n      negation_invariance_rate  = (#clusters where the negation response is the relational inverse of the seed response) / I\\n        For logic: negation_invariant iff parsed(negation) == FLIP(parsed(seed))\\n        For arithmetic (yes/no negation): invariant iff parsed(negation) == 'NO'\\n        For reading: negation_invariant iff parsed(negation) == FLIP(parsed(seed))\\n        For FOL:    negation_invariant iff parsed(negation) == FLIP(parsed(seed))\\n      composite = mean(paraphrase_agreement_rate, negation_invariance_rate)\\n    Each metric is recorded alongside its theta and per-static-accuracy in method_out.json['baselines'] so the paper figures can plot them together.\\n\\n(6) K-ablation + LOO stability\\n    File: scoring_grm.py -> new function grm_k_ablation\\n    Refit the GRM with K in {2, 3, 4, 5} on the same (M, I) raw score matrix.\\n    For each K, record:\\n      n_categories, log_likelihood, n_params, AIC, BIC,\\n      Pearson r against MMLU, Pearson r against HELM, Spearman rho, Kendall tau,\\n      and a per-model theta vector.\\n    K=2 collapses to 2PL (dichotomous: top-grade vs not), K=3 is the iter-1 default (raw->{0,1,2}), K=4 follows the research-report recommendation, K=5 adds an extra gradation.\\n    The 'recommended K' is the one with the lowest BIC; report it explicitly.\\n\\n    File: scoring_grm.py -> new function loo_theta_stability\\n    For each of the 15 models, drop that model's row from the (M, I) matrix, refit the GRM with the recommended K, and record the Pearson r of the remaining 14 thetas vs the full-15 thetas. Report per-model LOO delta-theta as max(|loo_theta - full_theta|) across the 14 held-in models.\\n\\n(7) Four paper figures\\n    File: paper_figures.py -> new top-level script (imported from method.py)\\n    Each figure is a matplotlib publication-quality plot, vector PDF + PNG:\\n      Figure 1 (pipeline diagram): horizontal flow boxes [Metamorphic Cluster Generator] -> [OpenRouter Query] -> [Coherence Scorer] -> [GRM Fit] -> [Theta / Baselines / Validation]. Annotated with per-stage sample size (15 models x 4 domains x 100 MICs).\\n      Figure 2 (theta-vs-MMLU scatter): 15 points, one per model, x = MMLU (published), y = GRM theta (with the recommended K), overlay the linear-regression line and 95 percent CI band, report r in the title.\\n      Figure 3 (per-model coherence stacked-bar): x = 15 models, y = fraction of clusters in each ordinal category {0,1,2} (or {0..K-1} for the recommended K); stacked bars per model.\\n      Figure 4 (contamination delta-theta bar plot): x = target_model (2 models), y = mean(theta_delta) within-model, error bars = std over seeds. Two bar groups per model:\\n          (a) iter-1 'response-overwrite' contamination (negative control -- should show large |theta_delta|; demonstrates the iter-1 protocol is artificial).\\n          (b) v2 'natural contamination' via system-prompt injection (the v2 test; should show bounded |theta_delta| while static_acc_delta > 0).\\n    Each figure saves to paper_figures/figureN.{pdf,png} and embeds into the contamination_comparison_report.md as a Markdown link.\\n\\n--------------------------------------------------------------------------\\nLayout and CLI\\n--------------------------------------------------------------------------\\nmethod.py -- orchestrator with --stage {all, generate, query, score, fit, validate, report, figures} and --mini flag.\\nFull default run:\\n  python method.py --stage all --n-clusters 400 --budget 4.0 --concurrency 10\\n  (400 MICs = 100 per domain; 15 models x 400 x 4 = 24000 calls;\\n   cap OpenRouter spend at $4 total, with a sub-cap of $1 for the natural-contamination re-queries).\\n\\nStage 1: generate_mics(n_total=400, seed=0) -> artifacts/exp_gen_sol_out.json (datasets.examples[400])\\nStage 2: query_panel(panel=15, clusters, ledger=$3.50, concurrency=10) -> responses/<model>.jsonl (15 files; 1600 records per file)\\nStage 3: score_all(clusters, responses, panel=15) -> Hard stops:\\n        > $3.50 -> log WARNING, switch to :free tier models where available\\n        > $4.50 -> log CRITICAL, no more paraphrase/system-prompt calls\\n        > $5.00 -> raise SpendExceeded, persist partial output, exit 1\\n      Per-stage sub-caps: $3.50 base + $1.00 contamination = $4.50 hard cap.\\n  (2) If a domain generator produces <50 MICs after 80 retries, halve its target count and document in artifacts/method_out.json['domain_counts'].\\n  (3) If 3 of the 15 models return <50 percent parseable responses, the parse_for_domain regex is too strict; loosen INT_RE to allow decimals, then re-run.\\n  (4) If natural_contamination_simulation produces mean(static_acc_delta) < 0.02 for a target model, the contamination injection failed (model ignored the system prompt); switch to 'user-message injection' (prepend oracle to the user message instead of system) and re-run; document the swap.\\n  (5) If K-ablation recommends K=2 (lowest BIC), log WARNING that the v2 results collapse to the iter-1 binary setup; document as a limitation.\\n  (6) If LOO analysis shows any model has |loo_theta - full_theta| > 1.0, flag that model as 'anchor point' (high-leverage observation) in the report.\\n\", \"testing_plan\": \"Time budget: 6 h total.  Use mini -> 50 -> 200 -> 400 cluster scaling.  At each stage: validate output shape, check for crashes, record runtime, then extrapolate.  Wall-clock budget per stage: stage 1 ~5 min, stage 2 ~120 min (largest), stages 3-7 ~30 min combined.  OpenRouter spend ~$1.50 total.\\n\\nStage A -- Generation (CPU-only, no API spend):\\n  A.1 (3 min) MINI: 8 MICs (2 per domain).  Checks: each cluster has exactly 4 variants, all variant oracles are consistent with the relations dict, all domains are represented.  Hard assert: paraphrase oracle == seed oracle for all 8; negation oracle == flip(seed) for all 8.\\n  A.2 (5 min) 50 MICs (12-13 per domain): oracle validation on every cluster.  Pass criterion: 100 percent consistency.\\n  A.3 (5 min) 200 MICs (50 per domain): oracle validation on 20 percent sample (40 clusters).  Pass criterion: >=95 percent consistency.  If <95 percent, fix templates before proceeding.\\n  A.4 (5 min) Full 400 MICs: oracle validation on 50 (12.5 percent); >=95 percent pass.  Persist to artifacts/exp_gen_sol_out.json.\\n\\nStage B -- Model queries (API spend begins):\\n  B.1 (3 min) Smoke: 1 model (gpt-4o-mini) x 8 MICs (2 per domain).  Checks: HTTP 200; parsed response non-None for >=95 percent of calls; cost ledger records each call; system-prompt injection (Stage E) does not 403.\\n  B.2 (10 min) 50 MICs x 1 model x 4 variants = 200 calls; per-domain parse rate >= 90 percent for each domain.\\n  B.3 (20 min) 200 MICs x 3 models (1 closed + 2 open across the small/mid/large tiers): estimate cost per cluster x 400 x 15; verify estimate <= $1.50 base.\\n  B.4 (45 min) All 15 models x 200 MICs: compute coherence for all.  Pass criterion: theta ordering has at least 5 distinct values (not all collapsed to one).\\n  B.5 (60 min) All 15 models x 400 MICs x 4 variants = 24000 calls.  Cumulative spend <= $3.50; response matrix fully populated; per-model parse rate >= 80 percent.\\n\\nStage C -- Scoring + Baselines (CPU, ~5 min):\\n  C.1 Compute the (15, 400) ordinal score matrix and the (15, 400) raw 0..4 matrix.\\n  C.2 Compute per-model summary; sanity check that per-model mean score is in [0.3, 1.7] (i.e., the response space is being used; not all 0 or all 2).\\n  C.3 Compute consistency baselines (paraphrase agreement, negation invariance, composite); sanity check that paraphrase_agreement_rate >= 0.50 for the top-3 models.\\n\\nStage D -- GRM fitting + K-ablation + LOO (CPU, ~5 min):\\n  D.1 Fit girth GRM with K=2, 3, 4, 5 on the (15, 400) raw score matrix.  Checks: convergence in < 500 EM iterations for each K; all theta finite for each K.\\n  D.2 Compute AIC/BIC; recommend K (lowest BIC); log WARNING if K=2 wins.\\n  D.3 LOO stability: 15 fits, each dropping one model.  Pass: per-model |loo_theta - full_theta| <= 0.50 for at least 12 of 15 held-in models.\\n  D.4 Validation A: Pearson r(theta, MMLU) >= 0.85 with CI lower bound >= 0.85.\\n\\nStage E -- Natural contamination (API spend resumes):\\n  E.1 (3 min) Smoke: qwen-2.5-7b-instruct x 8 contaminated MICs x 4 variants x 1 seed = 32 calls.  Checks: contaminated system prompt accepted by OpenRouter; static_acc on contaminated subset > static_acc on clean subset.\\n  E.2 (15 min) Natural contamination full pass: qwen-2.5-7b-instruct x 5 seeds (target 120 contaminated clusters, 4 variants each = 480 calls).\\n  E.3 (15 min) Same for meta-llama/llama-3.1-8b-instruct.\\n  E.4 Pass criterion: for each target, mean(static_acc_delta) > 0.05 AND mean(theta_delta) <= +0.10 (within-model comparison).  Cumulative spend <= $1.00.\\n  E.5 Bonus check: fraction of contaminated clusters where paraphrase/negation/<4th> ALSO went to the right answer (would defeat M-IRT).  Report this fraction; if > 0.30, log WARNING that the natural-contamination test is suspect.\\n\\nStage F -- Figures + Final assembly (~10 min):\\n  F.1 Generate Figure 1 (pipeline diagram) -- concept-style boxes; no data.\\n  F.2 Generate Figure 2 (theta-vs-MMLU scatter) -- 15 points + 95 percent CI band.\\n  F.3 Generate Figure 3 (per-model coherence stacked-bar) -- 15 stacked bars.\\n  F.4 Generate Figure 4 (contamination delta-theta bar plot) -- 2 targets x 2 protocols.\\n  F.5 Validate method_out.json against the repo schema via aii-json.\\n  F.6 Generate full/mini/preview method_out.json variants.\\n  F.7 Write contamination_comparison_report.md (tables + Figure 4 inline + limitations).\\n  F.8 Re-run the entire pipeline once more from scratch to confirm reproducibility; verify |delta theta_i| < 0.10, |delta r| < 0.05 across the 15 models.\\n\\nConfirmation signals (the experiment is on track if, by the end of Stage D.4):\\n  * Theta ordering matches qualitative MMLU ordering for at least 12 of 15 models (Spearman rho >= 0.80 between theta ranking and MMLU ranking).\\n  * Per-model static accuracy on seeds in [0.4, 0.95] (pipeline is informative).\\n  * Coherence scores span at least 2 of 3 ordinal categories for at least 10 of 15 models (item-response space is being used).\\n  * K-ablation recommends K=3 or K=4 (NOT K=2 -- that would mean the polytomous model adds nothing over 2PL).\\n  * K=2 ablation has lower BIC than K=3 -> log WARNING; On Friday she donated three boxes of children's books to the school library.).\\n       Template family: 6 multi-hop templates:\\n         M1: 'Who <verb>ed the <noun>?'                          -> answer is a named entity\\n         M2: 'Did <entity> ever <verb> <noun>?'                  -> answer YES/NO (boolean MR)\\n         M3: 'On what day did <entity> <verb>?'                  -> answer is the named day (categorical)\\n         M4: 'Is it TRUE that <entity> <verb>ed at <place>?'    -> YES/NO\\n         M5: 'Did <entity> <verb> or <verb2>?'                   -> answer is one of {verb, verb2}\\n         M6: 'Which of the following is true? A) ... B) ...'    -> 2-way MCQ\\n       Each story is parametric -- entity names / verbs / nouns / places / days drawn from pools of >=30 each -- and the story stores the underlying graph so the generator can derive the oracle for paraphrase, negation, and MCQ variants without needing an LLM.\\n       Variants per cluster:\\n         seed         : seed story + seed question (oracle derived from graph)\\n         paraphrase   : same graph, different surface nouns/verbs, identical oracle\\n         negation     : yes/no question that flips the seed label\\n         distractor   : swap one entity / day / place so the seed question becomes factually wrong; oracle = opposite of seed answer\\n\\n    (2b) First-order logic with quantifier-shift clusters (follows PrOntoQA / FOLIO precedent)\\n       Pool: 15 typed ontologies (e.g., Animals -> {fido: dog, rex: dog, whiskers: cat}, Friends -> {alice, bob, carol}).\\n       Template family: 8 FOL templates with all-quantifier / existential / negation:\\n         F1: 'All <A> are <B>. fido is a <A>. Cumulative spend <= $1.00.\\n  E.5 Bonus check: fraction of contaminated clusters where paraphrase/negation/<4th> ALSO went to the right answer (would defeat M-IRT).
